<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For each $`0\lt \kappa\lt 8`$, we construct a deterministic Hausdorff gauge that almost surely assigns positive finite measure to every nontrivial compact positive-time segment of chordal Schramm–Loewner evolution. This answers Schramm's Hausdorff-measure existence problem in this parameter range. The entire trace has finite expected gauge measure in each bounded box.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Fix $0<\kappa<8$, and let $\gamma$ be chordal $\mathrm{SLE}_\kappa$ from $0$ to $\infty$ in the upper half-plane $\mathbb H$, parametrized by half-plane capacity $2t$. There is a deterministic Hausdorff gauge $h_\kappa$, depending only on $\kappa$, such that almost surely, simultaneously for every real $0<s<t<\infty$, $$0<\mathcal H^{h_\kappa}(\gamma([s,t]))<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: A deterministic gauge assigns positive finite Hausdorff measure simultaneously to every stated positive-time trace segment.
- **C2** (construction, [source](#stmt-op-finite-tilt)): For every finite $s\ge0$, the nonnegative weight $$M_s(x)=\frac{G_{\tau_s(x)}(x)}{G_0(x)} \mathbf 1_{\{\tau_s(x)<\infty\}} \label{op:finite-density}$$ has expectation one. It defines a law $\mathbb P_x^*$ on the path stopped at $\tau_s(x)$, consistently as $s$ increases. Under these laws, the angle in inner time solves $$\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta_s=\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}W_s+2a\cot\theta_s\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s, \qquad \mathcal L^*=\tfrac12\partial_\theta^2 +2a\cot\theta\,\partial_\theta, \label{op:tilted-angle}$$ and neither endpoint is reached in finite inner time.* *For an arbitrary stopping time $S$, on $\{S<\infty\}$ one has $$\mathbb E\!\left[G_{\tau_s(x)}(x) \mathbf 1_{\{\tau_s(x)<\infty,\ S<\tau_s(x)\}} \mid\mathcal F_S\right] =G_S(x)\mathbf 1_{\{x\in D_S,\ s_x(S)<s\}}. \label{op:conditional-weight}$$ Conditionally on such a surviving state, the remainder of the centered law is the finite stopped change of law in the domain $D_S$, with remaining inner duration $s-s_x(S)$. Indeed, $$R_S(x)e^{-2a(s-s_x(S))}=R_0(x)e^{-2as},$$ so the restarted target is the original radius level.
  - Explanation: Finite radius levels define consistent mean-one centered laws; their restart formula uses the remaining inner duration.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-op-mixing)): Let $$\pi_a(\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta)=c_a\sin^{4a}\theta\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta, \qquad c_a^{-1}=\int_0^\pi\sin^{4a}\theta\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta. \label{op:stationary}$$ There is a deterministic $s_a<\infty$ such that, for all $s\ge s_a$ and every initial angle in $(0,\pi)$, the transition law of <a href="#op:tilted-angle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="op:tilted-angle">[op:tilted-angle]</a> has density between $1/2$ and $2$ relative to $\pi_a$. In particular, after such a gap the conditional expectation of any nonnegative angle function lies between one half and twice its $\pi_a$ integral. Moreover, $$\int_0^\pi\sin^{-p}\theta\,\pi_a(\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta) =c_a\int_0^\pi\sin\theta\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta<\infty. \label{op:inverse-angle}$$
  - Explanation: The centered angle diffusion has a uniform stationary-density comparison after a deterministic mixing gap.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-op-potential)): Under the consistent centered laws, $T^*(x)=\lim_{s\to\infty}\tau_s(x)$ is finite almost surely. For every finite deterministic $t$ and bounded raw $\mathcal F_t$-measurable random variable $H$, $$\mathbb E[H G_t(x)] =G_0(x)\mathbb E_x^*[H\mathbf 1_{\{t<T^*(x)\}}]. \label{op:tested-potential}$$ On the right, $H$ is evaluated only on stopped data extending beyond $t$. Thus $$\mathbb EG_t(x)=G_0(x)\mathbb P_x^*(T^*(x)>t)\longrightarrow0 \quad(t\to\infty). \label{op:potential-decay}$$ For every stopping time $S$, $$\mathbb E[G_S(x)\mathbf 1_{\{S<\infty\}}]\le G_0(x). \label{op:optional-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Centered physical lifetime is almost surely finite, and tested Green potentials equal expectations on stopped data before that lifetime.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-mp-estimate)): Let $D=J(\mathbb H)$ as in Section <a href="#op:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="op:section">2</a>, and let $x_1,\ldots,x_m\in D$ be distinct. Suppose $$0<b_i\le R_D(x_i),\qquad b_i\le \|x_i-x_j\|\quad(j\ne i).$$ For targets $0<e_i\le b_i$, let $E$ be the event that each $x_i$ reaches conformal radius $e_i$ while alive. Then $$\mathbb P(E)\le C_{m,a}\prod_{i=1}^m(e_i/b_i)^\alpha. \label{mp:hit-bound}$$ The constant depends only on $m$ and $a$, independently of $D$, $J$, the initial angles, the radii, and the order and times of the hits. A point need not remain alive after reaching its own target. For targets exceeding some caps, the corresponding constraints may be dropped, giving, after enlarging $C_{m,a}$, $$\mathbb P(E)\le C_{m,a} \prod_{i=1}^m\min\{1,(e_i/b_i)^\alpha\}. \label{mp:capped-hit-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Distinct points may reach their own radius targets in any order; completed points need not survive later hits.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-mp-kernel-integral)): Let $0<\alpha<1$, $b>0$, and let $K\subset\mathbb C$ be a bounded Borel set. For distinct $x_1,\ldots,x_m$ set $$b_i=\min\bigl(b,\min_{j\ne i}\|x_i-x_j\|\bigr),\qquad F_b(x_1,\ldots,x_m)=\prod_{i=1}^m b_i^{-\alpha},$$ where the inner minimum is omitted when $m=1$. Then $$\int_{K^m}F_b(x_1,\ldots,x_m)\prod_i\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}A(x_i)<\infty. \label{mp:all-variable-integral}$$ The same assertion holds with one point fixed: for bounded $K_0$, $$\sup_{x_0\in K_0}\int_{K^m} F_b(x_0,x_1,\ldots,x_m)\prod_{i=1}^m\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}A(x_i)<\infty. \label{mp:fixed-center-integral}$$ Values on diagonals may be set to infinity; they do not affect these integrals.
  - Explanation: Capped collision kernels are integrable on bounded planar sets, including uniformly over a bounded fixed center.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-mp-level-moments)): Let $f\ge0$ be bounded and supported in a compact $K\Subset D$. Then, for every integer $m\ge1$, $$\sup_{l>0}\mathbb E[A_l(f)^m]<\infty. \label{mp:ordinary-moments}$$ Fix also $x_0\in D$ and $0<e_0\le R_D(x_0)$, and let $E_0$ be the event that $x_0$ reaches radius $e_0$ while alive. Then $$\sup_{l>0}\mathbb E[\mathbf 1_{E_0} A_l(f)^m]\le C e_0^\alpha. \label{mp:center-moments}$$ Here $C$ can be chosen using only $m,a$, the size and support of $f$, the bounded location of $x_0$, and a positive lower bound for $R_D$ on $K\cup\{x_0\}$. In particular, in a fixed disk these bounds are uniform over all initial boundary angles.
  - Explanation: Integrated level weights have compact moments uniformly over radius levels, with a center-hit factor when an additional center is prescribed.
- **C8** (construction, [source](#stmt-mass-construction)): There is an adapted increasing family $(\mu_t)_{0\le t\le\infty}$ of locally finite measures on $\mathbb H$, starting at zero, with the following properties. Its terminal measure is nonatomic and has moments of every integer order on compact sets. Almost surely, simultaneously for all $0\le v<t<\infty$, the increment $\mu_t-\mu_v$ is carried by $\gamma([v,t])\cap\mathbb H$. The process is locally continuous in total variation, and $\mu_t\uparrow\mu_\infty$ as $t\to\infty$. For every stopping time $S$, possibly infinite, $$\mathbb E\bigl[\mu_\infty(\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x)-\mu_S(\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x)\mid\mathcal F_S\bigr] =G_S(x)\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}A(x), \qquad G_\infty:=0. \label{mass:kernel}$$ This is an equality of conditional measure kernels; in particular it allows nonnegative tests known at time $S$. Moreover, $$\mathbb E\mu_\infty(\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x)=G_0(x)\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}A(x). \label{mass:mean}$$
  - Explanation: One increasing trace-mass process has a remaining conditional Green kernel, compact moments and nonatomic terminal mass.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mass-first-visit)): Put $v(x)=\inf\{t\ge0:\gamma(t)=x\}$, with value infinity if the set is empty. Almost surely $v(x)<\infty$ for $\mu_\infty$-almost every $x$, and simultaneously for every finite $t$, $$\mu_t(\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x)=\mathbf 1_{\{v(x)\le t\}}\mu_\infty(\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}x). \label{mass:first-visit-identity}$$
  - Explanation: Mass accumulated by a finite time is exactly terminal mass restricted to points already visited.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mass-restart)): Fix a stopping time $S$. On $\{S<\infty\}$ put $J_S=(g_S-B_S)^{-1}:\mathbb H\to H_S$, and let $\widetilde\mu$ be the canonical mass functional of the fresh Brownian motion $B_{S+t}-B_S$. Almost surely, as measures on $H_S$, simultaneously for all $0\le t\le\infty$, $$(\mu_{S+t}-\mu_S)\|_{H_S} =(J_S)_*\bigl(\|J_S'\|^d\widetilde\mu_t\bigr). \label{mass:restart-identity}$$ The same assertion holds when the initial domain is a fixed conformal image of $\mathbb H$. Once this equality of measures has been obtained, one may integrate any random nonnegative Borel weight on the restart domain and evaluate at any later random time.
  - Explanation: The actual future interior mass agrees pathwise with the conformally transported canonical fresh-driver mass at all later times.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mass-positive)): Almost surely, for every $0\le v<t<\infty$, $$(\mu_t-\mu_v)(\mathbb H)>0.$$ The positive mass can always be detected on an interior compact. The same statement holds in every fixed conformal image, in its associated half-plane time.
  - Explanation: Every nontrivial finite interval has positive increment mass detectable on an interior compact.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-coef-positive-moments)): For every integer $n\ge1$, the deterministic numbers $$m_n(\ell):=\mathbb E_{\mathrm{mix}}^*L_\ell^n, \qquad m_n:=\mathbb E_{\mathrm{mix}}^*L^n =\lim_{\ell\to\infty}m_n(\ell) \quad\text{satisfy}\quad 0<m_n<\infty. \label{coef:moments}$$ Moreover $L>0$ almost surely, and, for each fixed angle, $$\sup_{\ell>0}\mathbb E_\theta^*L_\ell^n \le C_{n,a}\sin^{-p}\theta. \label{coef:angle-moments}$$
  - Explanation: The mixed centered disk mass is positive and has finite moments of every integer order.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-coef-recovery)): For every fixed $\theta\in(0,\pi)$ and finite $\ell>0$, $$D_h(\theta,\cdot;\ell)\longrightarrow A_{\tau_\ell} \quad\text{in }L^2(\mathbb P_\theta).$$
  - Explanation: Discrete potential compensators recover mass at a random radius level in mean square.
- **C14** (construction, [source](#stmt-coef-explicit)): The moments in <a href="#coef:moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="coef:moments">[coef:moments]</a> are given by $$\begin{aligned} m_n(\ell) &=\lim_{u\to\infty}\lim_{h\to\infty} \int_0^\pi\int P_\ell(\theta,b) \bigl([D_h(\theta,b;\ell)]_0^u\bigr)^n \,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}W(b)\,\pi_a(\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta),\\ m_n&=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}m_n(\ell). \end{aligned} \label{coef:formula}$$ All limit indices may be restricted to positive integers. Thus these coefficients depend only on $\kappa$ and the specified Gaussian, angle and area integrals.
  - Explanation: Iterated Wiener, angle and area integrals specify the disk moments deterministically.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-gauge-admissible)): The function $h_\kappa$ is a Hausdorff gauge and satisfies $$\label{gauge:doubling} h_\kappa(Cr)\le C^2h_\kappa(r) \qquad(C\ge1,\ r\ge0).$$ The radii from distinct batches may interleave. Set $$H_{ni}(r)=b_{ni}r_{ni}^{\,d}\max\{1,(r/r_{ni})^2\}.$$ If $0\le F(r)\le M<\infty$ and $F(r)\le C H_{ni}(r)$ for every $n\ge n_0$, every $1\le i\le I_n$, and every $r>0$, then $F(r)\le C' h_\kappa(r)$ for all $r>0$, for some finite $C'$.
  - Explanation: The full envelope is an admissible gauge; bounded measures controlled by all late entries remain controlled after restoring finitely many entries.
- **C16** (bound, [source](#stmt-gauge-multiplicity)): The non-cleanup multiplicities satisfy, for every $n\ge1$, $$\label{gauge:upper-tail} \sum_{k\in\mathbb Z}N_{nk}\, \mathbb P^*_{\mathrm{mix}}\{L\ge16A_k\} \le\frac{4^n16^{-n}}{1-2^{-n}}.$$ For $n\ge3$, they also satisfy $$\label{gauge:success} \sum_{k\in\mathbb Z}N_{nk}\, \mathbb P^*_{\mathrm{mix}}\{L_{\ell_n}\ge A_k\} \ge 2^{n-2}-\frac{1}{2(1-2^{-n})} \ge\frac{2^n}{8}.$$ Including the cleanup entries, $$\label{gauge:tail-summability} \sum_{n\ge1}\sum_{i=1}^{I_n} \mathbb P^*_{\mathrm{mix}}\{L\ge16b_{ni}\}<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Finite moment batches balance summable large-threshold tails against many moderately large-mass opportunities.
- **C17** (bound, [source](#stmt-hd-bad-cells)): Fix a compact set $E\Subset\mathbb H$. Set $$D=16(C_*')^d.$$ For an entry $(n,i)$, write $r=r_{ni}$, $b=b_{ni}$, and use the square grid of side $r$. Call a cell $Q$ bad when $\mu_\infty(Q)>2Dbr^d$. For every sufficiently large $n$, uniformly over all its entries and all cells meeting $E$, $$\mathbb E[\mu_\infty(Q);\ Q\text{ bad}] \le 4\left(\int_QG_0(z)\,\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}A(z)\right) \mathbb P^*_{\mathrm{mix}}(L\ge16b). \label{hd:bad-mass}$$ Moreover, almost surely, $\mu_\infty$-almost every point of $E$ belongs to no bad cell for any entry of any sufficiently late batch.
  - Explanation: Expected mass in overfull grid cells is summable across all sufficiently late batch entries.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hd-lower)): Almost surely, $$\mathcal H^h(\gamma([s,t]))>0 \qquad\text{for every real }0<s<t<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Every stated positive-time trace segment has positive Hausdorff gauge measure on one common event.
- **C19** (bound, [source](#stmt-hd-visit)): Let $Q$ be a closed square of side $r$, centered at $z=x+iy$, where $y>0$. If $y>4r$ and $Q\cap\Gamma\ne\varnothing$, then $z$ reaches conformal radius $8r$ while alive. For every $r>0$ and $y>0$, $$\mathbb P(Q\cap\Gamma\ne\varnothing) \le C_a r^\alpha y^{-\alpha}. \label{hd:one-cell}$$
  - Explanation: A sufficiently high visited square forces its center to cross a conformal-radius level; every positive height admits a visit-probability bound.
- **C20** (bound, [source](#stmt-hd-upper)): For every integer $m\ge1$, the random variable $\mathcal H^h(\Gamma\cap B_m)$, where $B_m=[-m,m]+i[0,m]$, is measurable and satisfies $$\mathbb E\,\mathcal H^h(\Gamma\cap B_m)<\infty. \label{hd:expected-box}$$ In particular, these whole-trace bounded-box measures are almost surely finite simultaneously for all $m$.
  - Explanation: Whole-trace gauge measure in each stated bounded box is measurable with finite expectation, including boundary trace points.

          "anchor": "stmt-op-potential",
          "exact_quote": "Under the consistent centered laws, $T^*(x)=\\lim_{s\\to\\infty}\\tau_s(x)$ is finite almost surely. For every finite deterministic $t$ and bounded raw $\\mathcal F_t$-measurable random variable $H$, $$\\mathbb E[H G_t(x)]\n =G_0(x)\\mathbb E_x^*[H\\mathbf 1_{\\{t<T^*(x)\\}}].\n \\label{op:tested-potential}$$ On the right, $H$ is evaluated only on stopped data extending beyond $t$. Thus $$\\mathbb EG_t(x)=G_0(x)\\mathbb P_x^*(T^*(x)>t)\\longrightarrow0\n \\quad(t\\to\\infty).\n \\label{op:potential-decay}$$ For every stopping time $S$, $$\\mathbb E[G_S(x)\\mathbf 1_{\\{S<\\infty\\}}]\\le G_0(x).\n \\label{op:optional-bound}$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-230.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "C4",

      "quantifiers": [

          "exact_quote": "Under the consistent centered laws, $T^*(x)=\\lim_{s\\to\\infty}\\tau_s(x)$ is finite almost surely. Thus $$\\mathbb EG_t(x)=G_0(x)\\mathbb P_x^*(T^*(x)>t)\\longrightarrow0\n \\quad(t\\to\\infty).\n \\label{op:potential-decay}$$ For every stopping time $S$, $$\\mathbb E[G_S(x)\\mathbf 1_{\\{S<\\infty\\}}]\\le G_0(x).\n \\label{op:optional-bound}$$",

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mp-estimate",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D=J(\\mathbb H)$ as in Section <a href=\"#op:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"op:section\">2</a>, and let $x_1,\\ldots,x_m\\in D$ be distinct. Suppose $$0<b_i\\le R_D(x_i),\\qquad b_i\\le |x_i-x_j|\\quad(j\\ne i).$$ For targets $0<e_i\\le b_i$, let $E$ be the event that each $x_i$ reaches conformal radius $e_i$ while alive. Then $$\\mathbb P(E)\\le C_{m,a}\\prod_{i=1}^m(e_i/b_i)^\\alpha.\n \\label{mp:hit-bound}$$ The constant depends only on $m$ and $a$, independently of $D$, $J$, the initial angles, the radii, and the order and times of the hits. A point need not remain alive after reaching its own target. For targets exceeding some caps, the corresponding constraints may be dropped, giving, after enlarging $C_{m,a}$, $$\\mathbb P(E)\\le C_{m,a}\n                \\prod_{i=1}^m\\min\\{1,(e_i/b_i)^\\alpha\\}.\n \\label{mp:capped-hit-bound}$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-230.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mp-kernel-integral",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0<\\alpha<1$, $b>0$, and let $K\\subset\\mathbb C$ be a bounded Borel set. For distinct $x_1,\\ldots,x_m$ set $$b_i=\\min\\bigl(b,\\min_{j\\ne i}|x_i-x_j|\\bigr),\\qquad\n F_b(x_1,\\ldots,x_m)=\\prod_{i=1}^m b_i^{-\\alpha},$$ where the inner minimum is omitted when $m=1$. Then $$\\int_{K^m}F_b(x_1,\\ldots,x_m)\\prod_i\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}A(x_i)<\\infty.\n \\label{mp:all-variable-integral}$$ The same assertion holds with one point fixed: for bounded $K_0$, $$\\sup_{x_0\\in K_0}\\int_{K^m}\n F_b(x_0,x_1,\\ldots,x_m)\\prod_{i=1}^m\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}A(x_i)<\\infty.\n \\label{mp:fixed-center-integral}$$ Values on diagonals may be set to infinity; they do not affect these integrals.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-230.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-mp-kernel-integral",
          "exact_quote": "Let $0<\\alpha<1$, $b>0$, and let $K\\subset\\mathbb C$ be a bounded Borel set. Then $$\\int_{K^m}F_b(x_1,\\ldots,x_m)\\prod_i\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}A(x_i)<\\infty.\n \\label{mp:all-variable-integral}$$ The same assertion holds with one point fixed: for bounded $K_0$, $$\\sup_{x_0\\in K_0}\\int_{K^m}\n F_b(x_0,x_1,\\ldots,x_m)\\prod_{i=1}^m\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}A(x_i)<\\infty.\n \\label{mp:fixed-center-integral}$$ Values on diagonals may be set to infinity; they do not affect these integrals.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-230.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mp-level-moments",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f\\ge0$ be bounded and supported in a compact $K\\Subset D$. Then, for every integer $m\\ge1$, $$\\sup_{l>0}\\mathbb E[A_l(f)^m]<\\infty.\n \\label{mp:ordinary-moments}$$ Fix also $x_0\\in D$ and $0<e_0\\le R_D(x_0)$, and let $E_0$ be the event that $x_0$ reaches radius $e_0$ while alive. Then $$\\sup_{l>0}\\mathbb E[\\mathbf 1_{E_0} A_l(f)^m]\\le C e_0^\\alpha.\n \\label{mp:center-moments}$$ Here $C$ can be chosen using only $m,a$, the size and support of $f$, the bounded location of $x_0$, and a positive lower bound for $R_D$ on $K\\cup\\{x_0\\}$. In particular, in a fixed disk these bounds are uniform over all initial boundary angles.",

      },

Then $$\\sup_{l>0}\\mathbb E[\\mathbf 1_{E_0} A_l(f)^m]\\le C e_0^\\alpha.\n \\label{mp:center-moments}$$ Here $C$ can be chosen using only $m,a$, the size and support of $f$, the bounded location of $x_0$, and a positive lower bound for $R_D$ on $K\\cup\\{x_0\\}$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mass-construction",
        "exact_quote": "There is an adapted increasing family $(\\mu_t)_{0\\le t\\le\\infty}$ of locally finite measures on $\\mathbb H$, starting at zero, with the following properties. Its terminal measure is nonatomic and has moments of every integer order on compact sets. Almost surely, simultaneously for all $0\\le v<t<\\infty$, the increment $\\mu_t-\\mu_v$ is carried by $\\gamma([v,t])\\cap\\mathbb H$. The process is locally continuous in total variation, and $\\mu_t\\uparrow\\mu_\\infty$ as $t\\to\\infty$. For every stopping time $S$, possibly infinite, $$\\mathbb E\\bigl[\\mu_\\infty(\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}x)-\\mu_S(\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}x)\\mid\\mathcal F_S\\bigr]\n   =G_S(x)\\,\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}A(x),\n \\qquad G_\\infty:=0.\n \\label{mass:kernel}$$ This is an equality of conditional measure kernels; in particular it allows nonnegative tests known at time $S$. Moreover, $$\\mathbb E\\mu_\\infty(\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}x)=G_0(x)\\,\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}A(x).\n \\label{mass:mean}$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-230.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-mass-construction",
          "exact_quote": "There is an adapted increasing family $(\\mu_t)_{0\\le t\\le\\infty}$ of locally finite measures on $\\mathbb H$, starting at zero, with the following properties. Almost surely, simultaneously for all $0\\le v<t<\\infty$, the increment $\\mu_t-\\mu_v$ is carried by $\\gamma([v,t])\\cap\\mathbb H$. The process is locally continuous in total variation, and $\\mu_t\\uparrow\\mu_\\infty$ as $t\\to\\infty$. For every stopping time $S$, possibly infinite, $$\\mathbb E\\bigl[\\mu_\\infty(\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}x)-\\mu_S(\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}x)\\mid\\mathcal F_S\\bigr]\n   =G_S(x)\\,\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}A(x),\n \\qquad G_\\infty:=0.\n \\label{mass:kernel}$$ This is an equality of conditional measure kernels; in particular it allows nonnegative tests known at time $S$. Moreover, $$\\mathbb E\\mu_\\infty(\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}x)=G_0(x)\\,\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}A(x).\n \\label{mass:mean}$$",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-230.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-mass-first-visit",
        "exact_quote": "Put $v(x)=\\inf\\{t\\ge0:\\gamma(t)=x\\}$, with value infinity if the set is empty. Almost surely $v(x)<\\infty$ for $\\mu_\\infty$-almost every $x$, and simultaneously for every finite $t$, $$\\mu_t(\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}x)=\\mathbf 1_{\\{v(x)\\le t\\}}\\mu_\\infty(\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}x).\n \\label{mass:first-visit-identity}$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-230.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-mass-first-visit",

      "exact_statement": "Put $v(x)=\\inf\\{t\\ge0:\\gamma(t)=x\\}$, with value infinity if the set is empty. Almost surely $v(x)<\\infty$ for $\\mu_\\infty$-almost every $x$, and simultaneously for every finite $t$, $$\\mu_t(\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}x)=\\mathbf 1_{\\{v(x)\\le t\\}}\\mu_\\infty(\\mathop{}\\!\\mathrm{d}x).\n \\label{mass:first-visit-identity}$$",
      "explanation": "Mass accumulated by a finite time is exactly terminal mass restricted to points already visited.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mass-restart",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a stopping time $S$. On $\\{S<\\infty\\}$ put $J_S=(g_S-B_S)^{-1}:\\mathbb H\\to H_S$, and let $\\widetilde\\mu$ be the canonical mass functional of the fresh Brownian motion $B_{S+t}-B_S$. Almost surely, as measures on $H_S$, simultaneously for all $0\\le t\\le\\infty$, $$(\\mu_{S+t}-\\mu_S)|_{H_S}\n       =(J_S)_*\\bigl(|J_S'|^d\\widetilde\\mu_t\\bigr).\n \\label{mass:restart-identity}$$ The same assertion holds when the initial domain is a fixed conformal image of $\\mathbb H$. Once this equality of measures has been obtained, one may integrate any random nonnegative Borel weight on the restart domain and evaluate at any later random time.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-230.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-coef-positive-moments",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $n\\ge1$, the deterministic numbers $$m_n(\\ell):=\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm{mix}}^*L_\\ell^n,\n \\qquad\n m_n:=\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm{mix}}^*L^n\n       =\\lim_{\\ell\\to\\infty}m_n(\\ell)\n       \\quad\\text{satisfy}\\quad 0<m_n<\\infty.\n \\label{coef:moments}$$ Moreover $L>0$ almost surely, and, for each fixed angle, $$\\sup_{\\ell>0}\\mathbb E_\\theta^*L_\\ell^n\n                  \\le C_{n,a}\\sin^{-p}\\theta.\n \\label{coef:angle-moments}$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-230.1"
      },

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-230.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-gauge-admissible",
        "exact_quote": "The function $h_\\kappa$ is a Hausdorff gauge and satisfies $$\\label{gauge:doubling}\nh_\\kappa(Cr)\\le C^2h_\\kappa(r)\n\\qquad(C\\ge1,\\ r\\ge0).$$ The radii from distinct batches may interleave. Set $$H_{ni}(r)=b_{ni}r_{ni}^{\\,d}\\max\\{1,(r/r_{ni})^2\\}.$$ If $0\\le F(r)\\le M<\\infty$ and $F(r)\\le C H_{ni}(r)$ for every $n\\ge n_0$, every $1\\le i\\le I_n$, and every $r>0$, then $F(r)\\le C' h_\\kappa(r)$ for all $r>0$, for some finite $C'$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-230.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-gauge-admissible",
          "exact_quote": "The function $h_\\kappa$ is a Hausdorff gauge and satisfies $$\\label{gauge:doubling}\nh_\\kappa(Cr)\\le C^2h_\\kappa(r)\n\\qquad(C\\ge1,\\ r\\ge0).$$ The radii from distinct batches may interleave.       "exact_statement": "The function $h_\\kappa$ is a Hausdorff gauge and satisfies $$\\label{gauge:doubling}\nh_\\kappa(Cr)\\le C^2h_\\kappa(r)\n\\qquad(C\\ge1,\\ r\\ge0).$$ The radii from distinct batches may interleave.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-gauge-multiplicity",
        "exact_quote": "The non-cleanup multiplicities satisfy, for every $n\\ge1$, $$\\label{gauge:upper-tail}\n\\sum_{k\\in\\mathbb Z}N_{nk}\\,\n\\mathbb P^*_{\\mathrm{mix}}\\{L\\ge16A_k\\}\n\\le\\frac{4^n16^{-n}}{1-2^{-n}}.$$ For $n\\ge3$, they also satisfy $$\\label{gauge:success}\n\\sum_{k\\in\\mathbb Z}N_{nk}\\,\n\\mathbb P^*_{\\mathrm{mix}}\\{L_{\\ell_n}\\ge A_k\\}\n\\ge 2^{n-2}-\\frac{1}{2(1-2^{-n})}\n\\ge\\frac{2^n}{8}.$$ Including the cleanup entries, $$\\label{gauge:tail-summability}\n\\sum_{n\\ge1}\\sum_{i=1}^{I_n}\n\\mathbb P^*_{\\mathrm{mix}}\\{L\\ge16b_{ni}\\}<\\infty.$$",

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-230.1"

      "exact_statement": "The non-cleanup multiplicities satisfy, for every $n\\ge1$, $$\\label{gauge:upper-tail}\n\\sum_{k\\in\\mathbb Z}N_{nk}\\,\n\\mathbb P^*_{\\mathrm{mix}}\\{L\\ge16A_k\\}\n\\le\\frac{4^n16^{-n}}{1-2^{-n}}.$$ For $n\\ge3$, they also satisfy $$\\label{gauge:success}\n\\sum_{k\\in\\mathbb Z}N_{nk}\\,\n\\mathbb P^*_{\\mathrm{mix}}\\{L_{\\ell_n}\\ge A_k\\}\n\\ge 2^{n-2}-\\frac{1}{2(1-2^{-n})}\n\\ge\\frac{2^n}{8}.$$ Including the cleanup entries, $$\\label{gauge:tail-summability}\n\\sum_{n\\ge1}\\sum_{i=1}^{I_n}\n\\mathbb P^*_{\\mathrm{mix}}\\{L\\ge16b_{ni}\\}<\\infty.$$",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Hausdorff gauge** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A *Hausdorff gauge* is a continuous nondecreasing function $h:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ with $h(0)=0$ and $h(r)>0$ for $r>0$.
- **Survival domain** ([source](#op:future-trace)): Write $H_t$ for the survival domain of <a href="#op:loewner" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="op:loewner">[op:loewner]</a>. It is the unbounded component of $\mathbb H\setminus\gamma([0,t])$, and $g_t:H_t\to\mathbb H$ is conformal.
- **Conformal radius** ([source](#op:koebe-growth)): For a simply connected domain $U$ and $x\in U$, our conformal radius is $R_U(x)=\|F'(0)\|$, where $F:\mathbb D\to U$ is conformal with $F(0)=x$.
- **First radius-level time** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For $s\ge0$, let $\tau_s(x)$ be the time at which the radius first attains $R_0(x)e^{-2as}$ while $x$ is alive; it is infinity if this never occurs. This is the inverse inner clock on successful paths.
- **Conformal trace mass** ([source](#mass:conformal)): For a fixed conformal identification $J:\mathbb H\to D$, define $$\mu_t^D=J_*\bigl(\|J'\|^d\mu_t\bigr). \label{mass:conformal}$$
- **Moment-batch gauge** ([source](#pm-h-24)): Assign the radii $$\label{gauge:radii} r_{ni}=\exp\{-n^2-2a(i-1)(\ell_n+n)\}, \qquad 1\le i\le I_n,$$ and define $$\label{gauge:definition} \boxed{\quad h_\kappa(r)= \inf_{\substack{n\ge1\\1\le i\le I_n}} b_{ni}r_{ni}^{\,d}\max\{1,(r/r_{ni})^2\} \quad(r>0),\qquad h_\kappa(0)=0. \quad}$$ The infimum includes every entry of every batch.

| Q15 | Non-cleanup upper-tail bound | ≤ | $\frac{4^n16^{-n}}{1-2^{-n}}$ | For every $n\ge1$. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-op-finite-tilt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-op-finite-tilt): Localized Green tilt and cutoff exhaustion -> Consistent finite stopped centered laws
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-op-mixing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-op-mixing): Orthogonal polynomial transition expansion -> Uniform stationary-density mixing
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-mp-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mp-estimate): Discounted regularized product with individual completion stops -> Domain-uniform radius-hit estimates
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-mass-construction) / [proof](#pm-h-16): Weak extraction followed by deterministic convex combinations -> One common trace-mass process
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-mass-restart) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mass-restart): Conditional-kernel uniqueness on a countable determining family -> Actual pathwise restart at all later times
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-coef-explicit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-coef-explicit): Recovery of stopped mass, truncation and iterated integration -> Deterministic moment prescription
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-gauge-multiplicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gauge-multiplicity): Dyadic threshold repetitions and geometric sums -> Summable tails and many success opportunities
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-hd-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hd-lower): Restrict mass to good cells, restore early entries and sum over arbitrary covers -> Positive arbitrary-cover gauge measure
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-hd-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hd-upper): Adapted failures, disjoint candidate charging and boundary cleanup -> Finite expected bounded-box gauge measure
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hd-upper-2): Intersect common events and use boundedness of finite-time traces -> Positive finite measure on all stated intervals

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-gauge**: A deterministic Hausdorff gauge assigns positive finite measure simultaneously to every stated positive-time trace segment.
- **I-restart**: The actual future interior mass is the conformally transported canonical mass of the fresh Brownian driver, simultaneously at all later times.
- **I-box**: Whole-trace Hausdorff gauge measure in each stated bounded box is measurable and has finite expectation.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-finite**: Treat a finite numerical truncation as a certified evaluation of the exact gauge. -> The infinite limits have no asserted finite computational error bound.
- **A-model**: Transfer the positive finite measure assertion to an arbitrary simulated path or workload trace. -> The trace law may fail the chordal SLE hypotheses.
- **A-identification**: Identify the moment-defined Hausdorff measure with natural parametrization or assert a log-log asymptotic. -> Neither identification nor that asymptotic is asserted.
- **A-independent**: Replace stopped-history conditioning by independent trials when estimating test failures. -> The proof controls conditional success on the actual stopped past without establishing independence.
- **A-infinite-law**: Extend the finite stopped change of law to the original completed infinite-future sigma field. -> Such absolute continuity is not asserted by consistency of stopped laws.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The deterministic disk moments and the full gauge require infinite limits and an infimum over every batch entry. The source explicitly states that no finite computational error bound is asserted. A finite certified evaluator for this exact gauge is ABSENT.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The exact-measure conclusion assumes chordal $\mathrm{SLE}_\kappa$ in $\mathbb H$ with $0<\kappa<8$. Blocked use: ['Transfer to arbitrary random curves, random walks or parameters outside the stated range.']
- **L2** (edge_case): The segment positivity conclusion is stated for $0<s<t<\infty$; extending it to other time sets needs a separate argument. Blocked use: ['Claiming segment positivity at the initial point or on arbitrary time sets.']
- **L3** (hypothesis): The exceptional event may depend on the fixed $\kappa$. Blocked use: ['Claiming a common probability-one event over all parameters.']
- **L4** (approximation): The coefficients have an infinite-limit prescription without an asserted finite computational error bound. Blocked use: ['Certified finite gauge evaluation, stopping criteria, runtime bounds or accuracy claims based only on truncation.']
- **L5** (unknown): A log-log asymptotic and identification of Hausdorff measure with natural parametrization are not asserted. Blocked use: ['Substitution of a log-log gauge or identification with natural parametrization.']
- **L6** (unknown): The asymptotic relation to the companion closed-form gauge remains undetermined. Blocked use: ['Treating the companion gauge as asymptotically equivalent to the moment-defined gauge.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): General conformal images are used for interior domain evolution without assuming a boundary extension. Blocked use: ['Transporting boundary trace statements through an arbitrary conformal map.']
- **L8** (formal_scope): Consistent centered laws concern finite stopped data and do not assert absolute continuity on the completed infinite-future sigma field. Blocked use: ['Changing law on arbitrary completed infinite-future events.']
- **L9** (unknown): No sharp growth estimate in the multipoint order $m$ is claimed. Blocked use: ['Order-uniform constants, sharp moment growth or complexity bounds inferred from the fixed-order estimate.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The lower-cover argument takes the full infimum over every batch entry. Blocked use: ['Replacing the full gauge by a truncated batch envelope without a separate bound.']
- **L11** (edge_case): Bounded-box expected gauge measure does not by itself assert finite expected measure of the unbounded entire trace. Blocked use: ['Claiming finite expected whole-plane trace measure.']
- **L12** (hypothesis): Conditional mass tests use the actual stopped past; independence is unnecessary and is not established by angle mixing. Blocked use: ['Replacing conditional mixing bounds with independence of tests or centers.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 230 (`lean/docs/230.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [An exact Hausdorff gauge for SLE: A moment-integral and finite-batch construction](../../preprints/An-exact-Hausdorff-gauge-for-SLE-September-25-2026/An-exact-Hausdorff-gauge-for-SLE-September-25-2026.pdf)
> - [An explicit exact Hausdorff gauge for SLE: A regular formula from quantitative tails and dense visits](../../preprints/An-explicit-exact-Hausdorff-gauge-for-SLE-September-26-2026/An-explicit-exact-Hausdorff-gauge-for-SLE-September-26-2026.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> For $0<\kappa<8$, put $d=1+\kappa/8$. The formalization proves that every continuous nondecreasing Hausdorff gauge agreeing with $h(r)=r^d(\log\log(1/r))^{(2-d)/2}$ at sufficiently small positive radii almost surely assigns positive measure to every nontrivial compact positive-time segment of ordinary chordal $\mathrm{SLE}_\kappa$.

Comparator source `SLELowerPositivity.lean` (3398 bytes, sha256 `f550800b9a60db513d4c9a2a85f710d0036a6a9b8f717ee0e64c38edd35f606a`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-! Positivity of the explicit Hausdorff gauge on all positive-time SLE segments. -/

noncomputable section
open Set Filter MeasureTheory
open scoped Topology ENNReal NNReal
namespace SLEExactGauge
universe v_lower

noncomputable def dimension (κ : ℝ) : ℝ := 1 + κ / 8

noncomputable def codimension (κ : ℝ) : ℝ := ((2 : ℕ) : ℝ) - dimension κ

def upperHalfPlane : Set ℂ := {z | 0 < z.im}

def survivingDomain (γ : ℝ≥0 → ℂ) (t : ℝ≥0) : Set ℂ :=
  {z | z ∈ upperHalfPlane \ (γ '' Icc 0 t) ∧
    ¬ Bornology.IsBounded (connectedComponentIn (upperHalfPlane \ (γ '' Icc 0 t)) z)}

def IsCapacityTwoWitness (U : ℝ≥0 → ℝ) (γ : ℝ≥0 → ℂ)
    (G F : ℝ≥0 → ℂ → ℂ) : Prop :=
  Continuous γ ∧ γ 0 = 0 ∧ (∀ t, 0 ≤ (γ t).im) ∧
    (∀ t, DifferentiableOn ℂ (G t) (survivingDomain γ t)) ∧
    (∀ t, DifferentiableOn ℂ (F t) upperHalfPlane) ∧
    (∀ t, MapsTo (G t) (survivingDomain γ t) upperHalfPlane) ∧
    (∀ t, MapsTo (F t) upperHalfPlane (survivingDomain γ t)) ∧
    (∀ t z, z ∈ survivingDomain γ t → F t (G t z) = z) ∧
    (∀ t z, z ∈ upperHalfPlane → G t (F t z) = z) ∧
    (∀ z ∈ upperHalfPlane, G 0 z = z) ∧
    (∀ t z, z ∈ survivingDomain γ t →
      HasDerivWithinAt (fun u : ℝ => G (Real.toNNReal u) z)
        (2 / (G t z - (U t : ℂ))) (Ici 0) (t : ℝ)) ∧
    (∀ t, Tendsto (fun z : ℂ => z * (G t z - z))
      (cocompact ℂ ⊓ 𝓟 (survivingDomain γ t)) (𝓝 (2 * (t : ℂ)))) ∧
    (∀ t, Tendsto (fun y : ℝ => F t ((U t : ℂ) + (y : ℂ) * Complex.I))
      (𝓝[>] 0) (𝓝 (γ t)))

def IsCapacityTwoTrace (U : ℝ≥0 → ℝ) (γ : ℝ≥0 → ℂ) : Prop :=
  ∃ G F : ℝ≥0 → ℂ → ℂ, IsCapacityTwoWitness U γ G F

def IsOrdinaryChordalSLE {Ω : Type*} [MeasurableSpace Ω]
    (κ : ℝ) (γ : Ω → ℝ≥0 → ℂ) (P : Measure Ω) : Prop :=
  (∀ t, Measurable (fun ω => γ ω t)) ∧
  ∃ B : ℝ≥0 → Ω → ℝ, ProbabilityTheory.IsBrownianReal B P ∧
    ∀ᵐ ω ∂P, IsCapacityTwoTrace (fun t => Real.sqrt κ * B t ω) (γ ω)

structure IsGauge (h : ℝ → ℝ) : Prop where
  continuousOn : ContinuousOn h (Ici 0)
  monotoneOn : MonotoneOn h (Ici 0)
  zero : h 0 = 0
  positive : ∀ r, 0 < r → 0 < h r

noncomputable def hFormula (κ r : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  r ^ dimension κ * (Real.log (Real.log (1 / r))) ^ (codimension κ / ((2 : ℕ) : ℝ))

def HasSmallRadiusFormula (h f : ℝ → ℝ) : Prop :=
  h =ᶠ[𝓝[>] 0] f

noncomputable def extendedGauge (h : ℝ → ℝ) (r : ℝ≥0∞) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ENNReal.ofReal (h r.toReal)

noncomputable def hausdorffGauge (h : ℝ → ℝ) : Measure ℂ :=
  Measure.mkMetric (extendedGauge h)

def segment (γ : ℝ≥0 → ℂ) (s t : ℝ≥0) : Set ℂ := γ '' Icc s t

def SourceLowerMainTarget : Prop :=
  ∀ (Ω : Type v_lower) (_ : MeasurableSpace Ω) (P : Measure Ω), IsProbabilityMeasure P →
  ∀ κ : ℝ, 0 < κ → κ < ((8 : ℕ) : ℝ) →
  ∀ (γ : Ω → ℝ≥0 → ℂ), IsOrdinaryChordalSLE κ γ P →
  ∀ h : ℝ → ℝ, IsGauge h → HasSmallRadiusFormula h (hFormula κ) →
    ∀ᵐ ω ∂P, ∀ s t : ℝ≥0, 0 < s → s < t →
      0 < hausdorffGauge h (segment (γ ω) s t)

theorem sourceLowerMain_proved : SourceLowerMainTarget := by
  sorry

end SLEExactGauge

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For each $`0\lt \kappa\lt 8`$, we construct a deterministic Hausdorff gauge that almost surely assigns positive finite measure to every nontrivial compact positive-time segment of chordal Schramm–Loewner evolution. This answers Schramm's Hausdorff-measure existence problem in this parameter range. The entire trace has finite expected gauge measure in each bounded box.
